She initially threw herself into the job with intense energy, volunteering for extra shifts, sketching elaborate redesigns after hours, and taking on additional mentoring at the community center, which her supervisors first interpreted as youthful enthusiasm. Over several months, however, her fluctuating mood and erratic availability began to interfere with safety, punctuality, and her ability to follow blueprints consistently, leading to a formal warning from her foreman and tension with coworkers who had relied on her. When she later swung into a prolonged period where she stopped showing up reliably to the job site or community center and confided to a fellow apprentice that she couldn’t sleep and her head \"wouldn’t slow down\" enough to function, the union training coordinator suggested she pause her apprenticeship and connected her with an employee assistance program, which facilitated an urgent psychiatric assessment.\n- Chief Complaint: My thoughts won’t slow down, and it’s gotten to the point where I can’t focus enough to do my work safely.\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to be obsessed with learning new tricks on the torch and helping the kids at the community center with their projects, but lately I just stare at my tools or my phone for hours and can’t make myself care enough to pick anything up or show up for them.\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): Most nights I either lie there wired until three or four in the morning with my brain buzzing, or I crash for twelve hours straight and still wake up feeling like I haven’t really slept, so I’m dragging or jittery on the job site and can’t get into any kind of normal rhythm.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): Some days I move so slow I’m just standing there in front of the parts bin trying to remember what I came for, and other days I’m pacing the apartment, tapping my feet, picking at my fingers, and I literally can’t sit through a safety meeting without bouncing out of my chair.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): When I’m down, I keep replaying every mistake I’ve made on that Miami project and convince myself I’m a screw‑up who’s putting everyone in danger and wasting the union’s time and money, like I don’t deserve this apprenticeship or even the help people are giving me.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): On the worst days I lie there before work thinking it’d be easier if I just didn’t wake up, and I’ve caught myself looking at the pill bottles and planning out how many it would take before I finally scared myself enough to call off and stay with my mom.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): I swing from feeling on top of the world and snapping selfies on the scaffold at 2 a.m. because I’m sure we’re building something historic, to blowing up at my foreman or my boyfriend over tiny comments, like I go from ‘this is amazing’ to ‘everyone’s against me’ in the same afternoon.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): When I’m up, I can’t stop moving—I’ll volunteer for double shifts, come home and start reorganizing the whole apartment, then jump online to sign up for extra certifications at like midnight because I feel like I have to be doing ten things at once.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): During those high stretches I honestly feel like I’m the only one on the crew who really gets the system we’re installing, like if they would just let me run with my ideas I could redesign the whole water setup for downtown Miami and maybe even get written up in some magazine.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): Lately when I get going, I just talk and talk—about new pipe layouts, side business ideas, random stuff from TikTok—and people have to cut me off because I can’t seem to shut up or slow down long enough to let anyone else get a word in.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): It feels like my brain is a busted compressor running full blast, with thoughts about the blueprints, money, my family, new inventions, all blasting through my head at once so fast that I can’t grab onto one long enough to focus on a weld or even follow a simple set of instructions.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): There were weeks where I’d leave the site wired, go straight to the community center to start new projects with three different kids, then come home and stay up half the night sketching alternate piping layouts and messaging people about starting my own company, like I couldn’t stop chasing new goals even as everything around me was falling apart.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: I’ve had to step back from my apprenticeship because my foreman said I wasn’t safe or reliable on site anymore, and I’ve basically disappeared from the community center and my friends because I can’t keep up with plans or trust my mood enough to show up.\n- duration: Looking back, this whole rollercoaster with the highs and crashes has been going on for about the last five months, ever since our crew got put on the big Miami project.\n- depressive_episode_duration: The heavy, drained, guilty part where I can’t care about work or the kids has She initially threw herself into the job with intense energy, volunteering for extra shifts, sketching elaborate redesigns after hours, and taking on additional mentoring at the community center, which her supervisors first interpreted as youthful enthusiasm. Over several months, however, her fluctuating mood and erratic availability began to interfere with safety, punctuality, and her ability to follow blueprints consistently, leading to a formal warning from her foreman and tension with coworkers who had relied on her. When she later swung into a prolonged period where she stopped showing up reliably to the job site or community center and confided to a fellow apprentice that she couldn’t sleep and her head \"wouldn’t slow down\" enough to function, the union training coordinator suggested she pause her apprenticeship and connected her with an employee assistance program, which facilitated an urgent psychiatric assessment.\n- Chief Complaint: My thoughts won’t slow down, and it’s gotten to the point where I can’t focus enough to do my work safely.\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to be obsessed with learning new tricks on the torch and helping the kids at the community center with their projects, but lately I just stare at my tools or my phone for hours and can’t make myself care enough to pick anything up or show up for them.\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): Most nights I either lie there wired until three or four in the morning with my brain buzzing, or I crash for twelve hours straight and still wake up feeling like I haven’t really slept, so I’m dragging or jittery on the job site and can’t get into any kind of normal rhythm.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): Some days I move so slow I’m just standing there in front of the parts bin trying to remember what I came for, and other days I’m pacing the apartment, tapping my feet, picking at my fingers, and I literally can’t sit through a safety meeting without bouncing out of my chair.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): When I’m down, I keep replaying every mistake I’ve made on that Miami project and convince myself I’m a screw‑up who’s putting everyone in danger and wasting the union’s time and money, like I don’t deserve this apprenticeship or even the help people are giving me.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): On the worst days I lie there before work thinking it’d be easier if I just didn’t wake up, and I’ve caught myself looking at the pill bottles and planning out how many it would take before I finally scared myself enough to call off and stay with my mom.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): I swing from feeling on top of the world and snapping selfies on the scaffold at 2 a.m. because I’m sure we’re building something historic, to blowing up at my foreman or my boyfriend over tiny comments, like I go from ‘this is amazing’ to ‘everyone’s against me’ in the same afternoon.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): When I’m up, I can’t stop moving—I’ll volunteer for double shifts, come home and start reorganizing the whole apartment, then jump online to sign up for extra certifications at like midnight because I feel like I have to be doing ten things at once.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): During those high stretches I honestly feel like I’m the only one on the crew who really gets the system we’re installing, like if they would just let me run with my ideas I could redesign the whole water setup for downtown Miami and maybe even get written up in some magazine.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): Lately when I get going, I just talk and talk—about new pipe layouts, side business ideas, random stuff from TikTok—and people have to cut me off because I can’t seem to shut up or slow down long enough to let anyone else get a word in.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): It feels like my brain is a busted compressor running full blast, with thoughts about the blueprints, money, my family, new inventions, all blasting through my head at once so fast that I can’t grab onto one long enough to focus on a weld or even follow a simple set of instructions.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): There were weeks where I’d leave the site wired, go straight to the community center to start new projects with three different kids, then come home and stay up half the night sketching alternate piping layouts and messaging people about starting my own company, like I couldn’t stop chasing new goals even as everything around me was falling apart.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: I’ve had to step back from my apprenticeship because my foreman said I wasn’t safe or reliable on site anymore, and I’ve basically disappeared from the community center and my friends because I can’t keep up with plans or trust my mood enough to show up.\n- duration: Looking back, this whole rollercoaster with the highs and crashes has been going on for about the last five months, ever since our crew got put on the big Miami project.\n- depressive_episode_duration: The heavy, drained, guilty part where I can’t care about work or the kids has buzzing, or I crash for twelve hours straight and still wake up feeling like I haven’t really slept, so I’m dragging or jittery on the job site and can’t get into any kind of normal rhythm.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): Some days I move so slow I’m just standing there in front of the parts bin trying to remember what I came for, and other days I’m pacing the apartment, tapping my feet, picking at my fingers, and I literally can’t sit through a safety meeting without bouncing out of my chair.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): When I’m down, I keep replaying every mistake I’ve made on that Miami project and convince myself I’m a screw‑up who’s putting everyone in danger and wasting the union’s time and money, like I don’t deserve this apprenticeship or even the help people are giving me.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): On the worst days I lie there before work thinking it’d be easier if I just didn’t wake up, and I’ve caught myself looking at the pill bottles and planning out how many it would take before I finally scared myself enough to call off and stay with my mom.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): I swing from feeling on top of the world and snapping selfies on the scaffold at 2 a.m. because I’m sure we’re building something historic, to blowing up at my foreman or my boyfriend over tiny comments, like I go from ‘this is amazing’ to ‘everyone’s against me’ in the same afternoon.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): When I’m up, I can’t stop moving—I’ll volunteer for double shifts, come home and start reorganizing the whole apartment, then jump online to sign up for extra certifications at like midnight because I feel like I have to be doing ten things at once.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): During those high stretches I honestly feel like I’m the only one on the crew who really gets the system we’re installing, like if they would just let me run with my ideas I could redesign the whole water setup for downtown Miami and maybe even get written up in some magazine.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): Lately when I get going, I just talk and talk—about new pipe layouts, side business ideas, random stuff from TikTok—and people have to cut me off because I can’t seem to shut up or slow down long enough to let anyone else get a word in.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): It feels like my brain is a busted compressor running full blast, with thoughts about the blueprints, money, my family, new inventions, all blasting through my head at once so fast that I can’t grab onto one long enough to focus on a weld or even follow a simple set of instructions.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): There were weeks where I’d leave the site wired, go straight to the community center to start new projects with three different kids, then come home and stay up half the night sketching alternate piping layouts and messaging people about starting my own company, like I couldn’t stop chasing new goals even as everything around me was falling apart.\n▸ Additional I've never been hospitalized, but it has caused serious trouble at work. My foreman gave me a formal warning for being unsafe and erratic, and I eventually had to step back from my apprenticeship."

All of this trouble with my focus, restlessness, and mood swings really started over the last five to nine months, right after our crew got assigned to a big project in downtown Miami.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few months, have you felt a persistent loss of interest or pleasure in the activities you usually enjoy, or do you find that your mood is primarily dominated by constant worry and tension?\n\nPatient: It’s definitely more of a heavy lack of interest and apathy rather than just constant worry. I used to be obsessed with learning new tricks on the welding torch and helping kids at the community center, but lately I just stare at my tools or my phone for hours and can't bring myself to care enough to show up.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any similar periods of feeling this low, apathetic, or unable to function in the past, perhaps several years ago or before this current project started?\n\nPatient: No, I've never experienced anything like this before in my life. These heavy feelings and not being able to function only started during this current project in Miami. Before this, I was always able to handle my work and stay on top of things.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your sleep patterns or appetite since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: My sleep has been completely thrown off—some nights I lie awake wired until 3 or 4 AM with my brain buzzing, while other times I crash for 12 hours straight and still wake up exhausted. I haven't really noticed any major changes in my appetite, though.\n\nDoctor: During those nights when your brain is buzzing and you stay awake until 3 or 4 AM, do you feel an unusual surge of energy, increased confidence, or a need to talk more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely—I get this massive surge of energy where I feel like I have to do ten things at once, like reorganizing my apartment or signing up for certifications at midnight. My confidence shoots way up too, and I honestly feel like I'm the only one who can redesign the whole Miami water system. When I get going like that, I talk constantly and so fast about my ideas that people have to cut me off because I just can't stop.\n\nDoctor: During these periods where you feel high energy and extreme confidence, how many days in a row do these symptoms typically last, and have they ever caused you to get into serious trouble or require hospitalization?\n\nPatient: These high-energy periods usually last for weeks at a time, as part of a rollercoaster that's been going on for about five months. I've never been hospitalized, but it has caused serious trouble at work. My foreman gave me a formal warning for being unsafe and erratic, and I eventually had to step back from my apprenticeship.\n\nDoctor: During these past five months, do you experience these periods of high energy and grandiosity as distinct 'high' phases that alternate with your 'low' periods, or are you experiencing symptoms of both high energy and low mood at the same time?\n\nPatient: They definitely feel like distinct phases on a rollercoaster rather than happening at the same time. I'll have these high stretches where I'm on top of the world and feel like I can redesign the whole Miami water system, and then I'll crash into a low period where I feel heavy, guilty, and completely drained. Even though my mood can swing quickly, they still feel like separate up and down phases.\n\nDoctor: During your most recent low period, did you experience any moments where you felt so hopeless or empty that you considered harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Yes, on the worst days I lie there before work thinking it would be easier if I just didn't wake up. I've even caught myself looking at my pill bottles and planning out how many it would take, which scared me enough to call off and stay with my mom."

All of this trouble with my focus, restlessness, and mood swings really started over the last five to nine months, right after our crew got assigned to a big project in downtown Miami.\n\nDoctor: Over these past few months, have you felt a persistent loss of interest or pleasure in the activities you usually enjoy, or do you find that your mood is primarily dominated by constant worry and tension?\n\nPatient: It’s definitely more of a heavy lack of interest and apathy rather than just constant worry. I used to be obsessed with learning new tricks on the welding torch and helping kids at the community center, but lately I just stare at my tools or my phone for hours and can't bring myself to care enough to show up.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any similar periods of feeling this low, apathetic, or unable to function in the past, perhaps several years ago or before this current project started?\n\nPatient: No, I've never experienced anything like this before in my life. These heavy feelings and not being able to function only started during this current project in Miami. Before this, I was always able to handle my work and stay on top of things.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your sleep patterns or appetite since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: My sleep has been completely thrown off—some nights I lie awake wired until 3 or 4 AM with my brain buzzing, while other times I crash for 12 hours straight and still wake up exhausted. I haven't really noticed any major changes in my appetite, though.\n\nDoctor: During those nights when your brain is buzzing and you stay awake until 3 or 4 AM, do you feel an unusual surge of energy, increased confidence, or a need to talk more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely—I get this massive surge of energy where I feel like I have to do ten things at once, like reorganizing my apartment or signing up for certifications at midnight. My confidence shoots way up too, and I honestly feel like I'm the only one who can redesign the whole Miami water system. When I get going like that, I talk constantly and so fast about my ideas that people have to cut me off because I just can't stop.\n\nDoctor: During these periods where you feel high energy and extreme confidence, how many days in a row do these symptoms typically last, and have they ever caused you to get into serious trouble or require hospitalization?\n\nPatient: These high-energy periods usually last for weeks at a time, as part of a rollercoaster that's been going on for about five months. I've never been hospitalized, but it has caused serious trouble at work. My foreman gave me a formal warning for being unsafe and erratic, and I eventually had to step back from my apprenticeship.\n\nDoctor: During these past five months, do you experience these periods of high energy and grandiosity as distinct 'high' phases that alternate with your 'low' periods, or are you experiencing symptoms of both high energy and low mood at the same time?\n\nPatient: They definitely feel like distinct phases on a rollercoaster rather than happening at the same time. I'll have these high stretches where I'm on top of the world and feel like I can redesign the whole Miami water system, and then I'll crash into a low period where I feel heavy, guilty, and completely drained. Even though my mood can swing quickly, they still feel like separate up and down phases.\n\nDoctor: During your most recent low period, did you experience any moments where you felt so hopeless or empty that you considered harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Yes, on the worst days I lie there before work thinking it would be easier if I just didn't wake up. I've even caught myself looking at my pill bottles and planning out how many it would take, which scared me enough to call off and stay with my mom.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, have you ever engaged in risky behaviors—such as spending excessive amounts of money, reckless driving, or other impulsive actions—that resulted in significant, long-lasting consequences for your life or safety?\n\nPatient: I haven't really done things like reckless driving or wild spending sprees. But I have done some slightly unsafe things, like taking selfies on a high scaffold at two in the morning when I was feeling on top of the world."

Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others. Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.
